A new **Queries** sub-tab inserted at index 6 â€” right after `Query Store by Duration`, matching the Dashboard's Query Store â†’ Query Store Regressions adjacency (every routing reference uses the NAMED constant, so only Expensive Queries / Query Heatmap shift, and the XAML `<TabItem>` order tracks the constants exactly â€” the same insert pattern as [#1515]). The read (`ViewerDataService.QueryStoreRegressions.cs`) is the Postgres port of the Dashboard's `report.query_store_regressions` inline TVF (`install/47_create_reporting_views.sql`) against `query_store_stats`: two windowed CTEs â€” BASELINE (every capture BEFORE the window) vs RECENT (the in-window captures) â€” averaged and joined per `(database, query_id)`, computing the duration / CPU / I/O regression percents, the execution-count-weighted `additional_duration_ms` (extra total time = per-exec duration delta Ã— recent exec count), the plan-count deltas, and a DURATION-driven severity band, keeping only rows whose **CPU regressed > 25%** (the TVF's single-metric gate), ranked by added duration and capped at the TVF's TOP (50). Faithful store adaptations, all documented in the read: the baseline/recent split windows on `collection_time` (Darling's UTC window column â€” the frame the toolbar's bounds are in) rather than the TVF's `server_last_execution_time` (Darling's column is the server's LOCAL wall clock, so windowing it against UTC bounds would be a timezone bug); the recent query-text sample is `MAX(query_text)` from Darling's already-decompressed column (the TVF `DECOMPRESS()`es a compressed one); and it ports the TVF's ACTUAL behavior (unbounded baseline, plain AVG, CPU-only gate), NOT the stale intent comment on the Dashboard's C# caller (bounded/mirrored baseline, weighted averages, multi-metric, minimums) that the TVF does not actually run. The row VM reproduces **every Dashboard grid column** (Last Execution, Severity, Database, Query ID, Total Impact, Base/Recent Execs, Base/Recent Plans, Duration Î”%, Base/Recent Dur, CPU Î”%, Base/Recent CPU, I/O Î”%, Base/Recent Reads, Query Text); the grid honors the global #1319 database filter (`$4::text[] IS NULL OR database_name = ANY($4)`), carries a `DataGridFilterManager`, and wires the per-row **double-click execution-history** window (reusing `QueryStoreHistoryWindow` query-scoped with plan_id 0, since the regression aggregates over plans â€” mirroring the Dashboard grid's double-click). Like the Dashboard grid it has NO slicer / comparison / plan surface (the read is a baseline-vs-recent contrast and carries no plan XML â€” like the sibling Query Store tab, whose View-Plan is deferred), so it uses the plan-less query-grid menu (`QueryGridRowStyle`) whose **Copy Repro** builds a plan-less repro from the query-text sample. Tests pin the read's load-bearing clauses (base table, the `collection_time` baseline/recent split, the CPU gate, the added-duration ranking + `LIMIT 50`, the severity thresholds, the bigint/integer casts, the Âµsâ†’ms conversion, the DB filter, and the PG positional dialect via the shared Queries dialect theory), the row display, the double-click key mapper, and Copy Repro, plus a `DARLING_TEST_PG`-gated live round-trip proving baseline-vs-recent detection, the CPU gate, the INNER-JOIN exclusion of NEW (no-baseline) queries, and the CRITICAL severity + added-duration math end-to-end. Darling-only â€” no Lite, Full Dashboard, or `install/*.sql` changes.
- **Darling viewer: live Current Active Queries sub-tab (service-mediated)** ([#1515]) â€” a LIVE, on-demand DMV snapshot of what is running on a monitored server RIGHT NOW, distinct from the stored **Active Queries** sub-tab (the collector's hourly snapshots). Darling's viewer is store-only, so it is delivered through the Stage-2 command plane exactly like `execute_actual_plan` / `fetch_plan`: the viewer enqueues a new `fetch_active_queries` command, the SERVICE (which holds a live connection to every monitored server) runs the query on the target's live connection and returns the rows in `result_json`, and the viewer renders them in a new **Current Active Queries** sub-tab (Queries sub-index 2, right after the stored Active Queries tab â€” every routing reference uses the named constant, so only the constant values shift) with a **Refresh** button (ON-DEMAND only â€” never auto-fetched on tab selection or a range refresh, since a live server hit is an explicit action). The DMV query is REUSED, not reinvented: a new read-only `DarlingCollectorRunner.FetchRowsAsync` (the "fetch phase only" twin of `RunAsync` â€” same `CollectorContext`, no store write) runs the shared `QuerySnapshotsCollector` against the live connection, so the live snapshot carries the SAME columns as the stored one and renders in the same `ViewerQuerySnapshotRow` grid (the Estimated / Actual plan buttons reuse the stored handlers on the plan the DMV read carried inline). New `CommandKind.FetchActiveQueries` is pinned viewerâ†”executor (`ViewerDataService.CommandFetchActiveQueries` â†” `DarlingCommandExecutor.ResolvePlan`), requires `target_server_id` and takes no args, dispatches through `IDarlingCommandHost.FetchActiveQueriesLiveAsync` â†’ `DarlingWorker.RunFetchActiveQueriesLiveAsync` (resolves the runtime under the servers lock, mirroring `RunFetchPlanAsync`), and returns legible `server not monitored` / `server not connected` / `timed out` / `permission denied` / `sql error` outcomes; the service caps the SQL read at 30s (comfortably under the viewer's 60s poll budget so a slow read reports a real outcome, not a poll miss), deletes the (large) command row after the viewer reads it, and â€” because enqueuing is a config write â€” a read-only `viewer` seat cannot fetch it (same rule as Pause / `fetch_plan`). It is a read-only DMV read (`sys.dm_exec_requests`/`sessions` + the sql_text / query_plan DMFs), so unlike `execute_actual_plan` it is NOT consent-class. Tests pin the command constant, the `ResolvePlan` case, and the FULL service-serialize â†’ viewer-parse wire contract (no live SQL Server needed), plus the timeout / failure mappings and the poll-budget-vs-SQL-cap ordering; the live executor round-trip is gated on `DARLING_TEST_PG` like the `fetch_plan` one. Darling-only â€” no Lite, Full Dashboard, or `install/*.sql` changes.
- **Lite viewer: Blocking Stats sub-tab (Tier-1 parity BUILD)** ([#1514]) â€” the Lite port of the Darling viewer's **Blocking Stats** sub-tab: a 2x2 chart grid + a window summary strip showing blocking AND deadlock SEVERITY over the window, appended as the LAST sub-tab of the **Blocking** tab (sub-index 4, after Deadlocks â€” nothing else reindexed; `RefreshBlockingAsync`'s subTabOnly switch gains `case 4` while the top-level Blocking tab stays index 8). It is the severity companion to Lite's existing Blocking **Trends** sub-tab (which plots blocking/deadlock COUNTS): the four charts plot block Max/Avg duration + block Total duration, then deadlock Max/Avg wait + deadlock Total wait (Total split onto its own axis per pair so the aggregate magnitude doesn't swamp the per-incident one), and the summary strip carries events / total / max / avg block duration, the deadlock count, and the deadlock victim count + total deadlock wait â€” the Lite equivalent of the Dashboard's pre-aggregated `collect.blocking_deadlock_stats` (`total_blocking_duration_ms` / `victim_count` / `total_deadlock_wait_time_ms`), computed ON-THE-FLY from rows the store already keeps, so no collector or migration is needed. The reader (`LocalDataService.BlockingStats.cs`) adapts Darling's `GetBlockingDurationStatsAsync` + `GetDeadlockSeverityStatsAsync` to DuckDB: the block-duration aggregate SUM/MAX/AVGs `wait_time_ms` per minute over the SAME XE-preferred (`v_blocked_process_reports`) + DMV-fallback (`v_dmv_blocking_snapshots`, `WHERE NOT EXISTS`) union the blocking-incident count trend uses â€” so the severity chart and the count chart never disagree on which rows exist â€” and honors the #1319 database filter on both arms; the deadlock-severity aggregate reads the raw `deadlock_graph_xml` over the SAME `v_deadlocks` / `collection_time` window as the deadlock count (uncapped, so it can't drop rows the count keeps), parses each graph OFF the UI thread with Lite's own `DeadlockProcessDetail.ParseFromRows` (the SAME parse the Deadlocks grid uses, so the two agree in-app), and buckets the per-process victim/wait rows by minute (`victim_count` = SUM of victims, total/max/avg over every process's wait). **Collapse check**: Darling's deadlock-severity computation is NOT a viewerâ†”service twin â€” the service/MCP side hosts only the count trends and deliberately omits a `get_blocking_deadlock_stats` (no raw victim-wait source), and the Darling viewer's aggregate already parses via the shared `PerformanceMonitor.Common.DeadlockGraphParser` â€” so per the brief Lite reuses its own existing `ParseFromRows` (keeping Lite's severity internally consistent with its Deadlocks grid) rather than introducing a third copy; nothing on the Darling side was touched. The four charts (`ServerTab.BlockingStats.cs`) mirror Darling's rendering with Lite's chart idioms (shared `ChartStyle` / `ChartHoverHelper`, `SeriesColors` / `ChartPalette` identities, `UtcOffsetMinutes` display conversion, Y-floor-at-0, window-pinned `SetLimitsX`, connected-scatter padded to the window ends), wired into the Blocking `TabControl` + `RefreshBlockingAsync` (both the subTabOnly `case 4` and the full-refresh path) + `ServerTab.xaml.cs` init/dispose. Real-DuckDB round-trip reader tests (`BlockingStatsReaderTests`) plant blocked_process_reports / dmv_blocking_snapshots / deadlocks rows and assert the per-minute duration aggregate (count + total/max/avg), the XEâ†’DMV fallback union, the #1319 filter, window bounding, and the deadlock-severity aggregate (victim_count + total/max/avg wait) reconciling with the deadlock count over the same window.
- **Lite viewer: Expensive Queries sub-tab (Tier-1 parity BUILD)** ([#1513]) â€” the Lite port of the Darling viewer's **Expensive Queries** view: a unified cross-source ranked list appended as the LAST sub-tab of the **Queries** tab (sub-index 6, after Query Heatmap â€” nothing else reindexed; the Queries inner switch gains `case 6` while the top-level Queries tab stays index 2). One grid UNIONs the three query engines Lite already collects â€” `query_stats` (Query Stats), `procedure_stats` (Stored Procedure / Trigger / Function), and `query_store_stats` (Query Store) â€” each grouped and TOP-20-per-source ranked by AVERAGE worker (CPU) time, then merged, re-ranked by average worker time and capped at 20, mirroring the Dashboard's `report.expensive_queries_today` and Darling's `GetUnifiedExpensiveQueriesAsync`. The reader (`LocalDataService.ExpensiveQueries.cs`) adapts the Postgres/T-SQL UNION to DuckDB against the `v_query_stats` / `v_procedure_stats` / `v_query_store_stats` archive views: the query_stats/query_store arms SUM the collected deltas / weight per-interval averages by `execution_count` (never the raw cumulative DMV totals), a LATERAL fetches each group's latest text + stored plan, every arm is both-sides window-bound on `collection_time` and honors the #1319 database filter, and the Âµsâ†’ms/sec + KB/pageâ†’MB unit conversions run in SQL exactly like the Dashboard view's outer projection. The stored plan rides IN-ROW, so "View Plan" / "Copy Repro Script" open it with zero extra reads via the shared `DataGridContextMenu` â€” mirroring the Top Queries sub-tab exactly (View Plan on the in-row plan; Get Actual Plan re-executes the runnable statement sources; both no-op cleanly for the module sources and for rows with no plan). Store-driven deviations from the Dashboard view, each faithful to Lite's store: Query Stats objects are the constant `Adhoc` (Lite's query_stats carries no object columns), all functions collapse to a single `Function` label (the shared collector labels them `FUNCTION`), the Query Store grant is pagesâ†’MB (Ã—8/1024, unit-correct, matching Lite's Query Store tab), and the Query Store arm carries neither a stored plan nor a plan_handle (Lite's query_store_stats stores neither) so its View Plan / Get Actual Plan gate off. Every Darling grid column is reproduced. Real-DuckDB round-trip reader tests (`ExpensiveQueriesReaderTests`) plant query_stats/procedure_stats/query_store rows and assert the cross-source ranking by avg worker time, the unit conversions, the source labels + object-name shapes, the in-row plan (and the Query Store no-plan/no-handle deviation), delta summing across snapshots, the #1319 filter, and window bounding.
- **Config-diff collapse to shared Common + Lite Configuration Changes tab (Tier-1 parity)** ([#1512]) â€” the Configuration Changes histories (server-config / database-config / trace-flag DRIFT, COMPUTED by diffing the store's append-only config snapshots â€” there is no change table) were HAND-DUPLICATED as a drift twin: the Darling viewer's `ViewerDataService.ConfigChanges` `Diff*` and the headless MCP host's `DarlingConfigHistoryReader` `Compute*`. **Part 1** hoists the one source into `PerformanceMonitor.Common.ConfigChangeDiff` â€” the three pure diff algorithms (server per-name value diff, database wide-row unpivot, trace-flag set-diff), the snapshot INPUT records, the change OUTPUT records carrying the PURE-DERIVED columns (`requires_restart`, `change_description`, `scope`, status text), and the 27 database-config setting names â€” then repoints both Darling sides: the viewer's `â€¦Row` view-models become thin DISPLAY wrappers that add only the timezone render (Darling's `ViewerTimeHelper`), and the service reader deletes its diff/records/setting-names and calls the shared class (the MCP tools pass `DateTime.MaxValue` as the no-op upper edge so the shared both-edges diff reproduces their prior unbounded-read behavior exactly). **Behavior-preserving** â€” the Darling config-history tests stay green (repointed to the shared class), no third copy. Same one-source pattern as the [#1507] significance collapse. **Part 2** builds Lite's **Configuration Changes** tab consuming the shared `ConfigChangeDiff` (never re-implemented) â€” a new LAST top-level tab (index 19, after System Events â€” nothing renumbered, so `RefreshVisibleTabAsync` switch `case 19` equals its 0-based XAML index) with three sub-tab GRIDS (Server Config / Database Config / Trace Flag changes) over the DuckDB snapshot views `v_server_config` / `v_database_config` / `v_trace_flags`. The read is upper-bounded at the window END (keeping the pre-window baseline snapshot so a change on the first in-window capture is still detected), the shared diff applies both edges, `capture_time` renders through `ServerTimeHelper.FormatServerTime`, the #1319 database filter is honored on the database-config diff, and **every Darling grid column is reproduced**. Real-DuckDB round-trip reader tests plant two captures encoding a known change and assert the diff (value move + derived columns, wide-row unpivot, trace-flag enable/disable/modify, pre-window baseline, out-of-window exclusion, the DB filter, and the row VM's server-time render), mirroring the Darling config-history scenarios.
- **Lite: connection-lost / connection-restored alerts, and a generic webhook channel for both apps** ([#1506]) â€” Lite was the only one of the three apps that could not tell you your server had gone down unless you happened to be watching the tray: the Dashboard emails on the connection edge and Darling's `DarlingSelfAlertEvaluator` emails *and* webhooks it, but Lite's edge fired a tray balloon and nothing else. Both edges in `MainWindow.CheckConnectionsAndNotify` now also route through Lite's alert path â€” email, webhook, and a `config_alert_log` row â€” as **"Server Unreachable"** / **"Server Restored"**, the metric names the shared `AlertSeverity` map and `AlertMetricClassifier` already understood (CRITICAL-red, and the restore classified as a green resolution), so no new naming was invented. The tray balloon is unchanged; this is purely additive. Delivery stays **edge-triggered** â€” the transition logic moved out of the inline WPF condition into a pure, unit-tested `ConnectionEdgeDetector`, so an eight-hour outage polled every minute produces ONE "Server Unreachable", not 480 â€” and it respects mute rules and per-server silencing exactly as Lite's other alerts do. Gated on the existing `App.NotifyConnectionChanges` preference (default on, already in Settings), which is also the name Darling uses; no second preference was added.
- Alongside it, a third webhook channel in the shared `WebhookAlertService` â€” **generic HTTP POST** â€” beside Teams and Slack, for **Lite and Darling**. It POSTs an operator-authored JSON body with `{{metric}}`, `{{server}}`, `{{value}}`, `{{threshold}}`, `{{severity}}`, `{{context}}` and `{{timestamp}}` placeholders and an arbitrary JSON headers object, which covers PagerDuty / Opsgenie / n8n and â€” the motivating use case â€” a GitHub `repository_dispatch` that re-runs a workflow when an alert fires. **This is deliberately not a script/exe runner:** arbitrary command execution in a signed binary is an EDR-flagged attack path, and a webhook reaches the same automation with no process-execution surface. Every substituted placeholder is **JSON-escaped** (via `JsonSerializer`, which â€” unlike `JsonEncodedText.Encode` â€” tolerates the lone surrogates SQL Server names can carry instead of throwing), so a server name carrying a quote or backslash â€” `HOST\INSTANCE`, or something hostile â€” cannot break out of the template and corrupt or inject into the posted JSON; the substituted body is validated as JSON before it goes on the wire, malformed headers JSON degrades to a clear logged error instead of throwing into the alert loop, and a `User-Agent` is defaulted (GitHub's API 403s requests without one). Settings live beside Teams/Slack in both apps' Settings windows with a "Send Test" button and a `repository_dispatch` example; the URL and the headers JSON are treated as **secrets** â€” Windows Credential Manager in Lite, and in Darling's Postgres control plane (schema **V26**) `generic_url` + `generic_headers` are column-`REVOKE`d from the read-only viewer role like the existing webhook URLs, since the headers carry the `Authorization` bearer token. The deprecated Dashboard satisfies the shared settings interface with the channel off and gains no new UI.

- **Lite viewer: System Events tab (Tier-1 parity BUILD â€” drains the coverage pin to ZERO)** ([#1509]) â€” the Lite port of the Darling viewer's **System Events** tab, the LAST unbuilt-UI pair on the parity board. Lite already collected `system_health_events` (raw `system_health` XE) and `default_trace_events` (built-in Default Trace) but had **no viewer surface** (the coverage pin from #1499 allow-listed both); this adds one, so `CollectorViewerCoverageTests.KnownStoreOnlyOrUnbuiltTables` is now **empty** â€” every collector table has a Lite reader and the ratchet is fully drained. New top-level tab (index 18, the new LAST tab, after Collection Health â€” nothing renumbered, so switch `case 18` still equals its 0-based XAML index) with 11 inner sub-tabs in Darling's order. The two lead sub-tabs â€” **Corruption Events** + **Contention Events** â€” are the SYSTEM-component counter CHARTS (bad pages / dump requests / access violations / write-AV; non-yielding tasks / latch warnings / sick spinlocks by type / SQL-vs-system CPU), all eight fed by ONE `sp_server_diagnostics` SYSTEM read with **no** significance filter (every snapshot plotted over time), in `ServerTab.SystemHealthCharts.cs` using Lite's chart idioms (shared `ChartStyle`/`ChartHoverHelper`, `SeriesColors`/`ChartPalette`, `UtcOffsetMinutes` display conversion, Y-floor-at-0). The remaining nine are parse-on-read GRIDS â€” Scheduler Issues, Severe Errors, Memory Conditions, Memory Broker, Memory Node OOM, Significant Waits, CPU Tasks, I/O Issues, Default Trace â€” with **every Darling grid column** reproduced (headers, bindings, formats). The reader (`LocalDataService.SystemEvents.cs`, mirroring Darling's `ViewerDataService.SystemEvents` reads adapted to DuckDB) fetches the raw `event_xml` for each category over the window from the `v_system_health_events` archive view, shreds it with the Common `SystemHealthParser`, and keeps the significant rows by calling the shared `PerformanceMonitor.Common.SystemHealthSignificance` **directly** (zero duplication â€” Lite and Darling share the one sp_HealthParser predicate source hoisted in #1507). The shred runs off the UI thread (the loaders wrap the reads in `Task.Run`, mirroring the deadlock-graph parse). Severe Errors resolves each event's `database_id` to a name from the latest `v_database_size_stats` snapshot (DuckDB `QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY database_id ORDER BY collection_time DESC)`, the equivalent of Darling's Postgres `DISTINCT ON`) and honors the #1319 database filter client-side on the resolved name; Default Trace honors it in SQL on `database_name`. The **Default Trace lane** reads `v_default_trace_events`, gates via the shared `DefaultTraceEventSignificance`, and â€” because the trace `StartTime` is the monitored server's LOCAL wall clock (not UTC like the XE `@timestamp`) â€” windows on the server-local bounds (`GetTimeRangeServerLocal`) and de-skews each row's `event_time` to naive-UTC (subtracting the connected server's `UtcOffsetMinutes`) so it shares the system_health rows' UTC frame and renders/sorts consistently through `ServerTimeHelper.FormatServerTime`. Wired into the TabControl + `RefreshVisibleTabAsync` switch + `MainTabControl_SelectionChanged` sub-tab-reload path + `ServerTab.xaml.cs` init/dispose, with a `DataGridFilterManager` on each of the nine grids. Real-DuckDB round-trip reader tests (`SystemEventsReaderTests`) plant fixture rows into the store and assert each category's significant set, the column projection, the DB-name resolution + #1319 filter, the event_time window bounding, and the Default Trace de-skew.
- **Lite viewer: CPU Scheduler, Plan Cache & Session Stats tabs (Tier-1 parity BUILD, batch of 3)** ([#1505]) â€” the Lite ports of three more Darling viewer per-server tabs whose data Lite already collected but never surfaced (the coverage pin from #1499 allow-listed `cpu_scheduler_stats` / `plan_cache_stats` / `session_summary_stats` as unbuilt UI). Placed together in the diagnostics tail right after Latches & Spinlocks â€” **CPU Scheduler** (index 14), **Plan Cache** (15), **Session Stats** (16), shifting Collection Health 14â†’17 with the `RefreshVisibleTabAsync` switch re-aligned so every top-level tab's 0-based index still equals its case. All three are **point-in-time snapshot** collectors (one aggregate row per collection, not cumulative-counter deltas), so the readers (`LocalDataService.CpuScheduler.cs` / `.PlanCache.cs` / `.SessionStats.cs`, mirroring Darling's `ViewerDataService` reads adapted to DuckDB against the `v_cpu_scheduler_stats` / `v_plan_cache_stats` / `v_session_summary_stats` archive views) plot the counts directly â€” no `LAG`/delta math. **CPU Scheduler**: a runnable / blocked / queued task-count trend over the window plus a latest-snapshot metric/value grid (schedulers, workers, NUMA, OS-memory pressure) whose warning rows highlight from the collector's own CASE-computed pressure flags, with the rolled-up pressure level + recommendation headline rows (`ClassifyCpuPressure`, install/47 `report.cpu_scheduler_pressure` parity). **Plan Cache**: a single-use vs multi-use plan-cache size (MB) trend (the single-use bloat signal, on the shared `SinglePagePlans`/`MultiPagePlans` palette keys), a summary strip with the TRUE total plans (uncapped SUM, independent of the top-30 grid), oldest-plan age, and a derived single-use bloat badge + forced-parameterization hint (`ClassifyPlanCacheBloat`, `report.plan_cache_bloat` parity), plus a filterable latest-snapshot composition grid per (cacheobjtype, objtype). **Session Stats**: a server-wide session-status trend (Total / Running / Sleeping / Background / Dormant / Idle >30m / Waiting for Memory, each on its fixed `Session*` palette key, a series only drawn when non-zero in the window so all-zero states stay out of the legend) plus a summary strip for the non-chartable attribution (top application / top host by connections + distinct databases). All three tabs mirror Darling's rendering with Lite's chart idioms (shared `ChartStyle`/`ChartHoverHelper`, `SeriesColors`/`ChartPalette`, Y-floor-at-0, `UtcOffsetMinutes` display conversion, `SetLimitsX` window-pinning), wired into the TabControl + switch + `ServerTab.xaml.cs` init/dispose, with the Plan Cache composition grid on a `DataGridFilterManager`. Removes the three tables from the coverage-pin allow-list (`CollectorViewerCoverageTests.KnownStoreOnlyOrUnbuiltTables`, now **2** â€” only `system_health_events` / `default_trace_events` remain), so the ratchet now REQUIRES their readers.
- **Lite viewer: Latches & Spinlocks tab (Tier-1 parity BUILD)** ([#1504]) â€” the Lite port of the Darling viewer's consolidated **Latches & Spinlocks** per-server tab. Lite already collected `latch_stats` (`sys.dm_os_latch_stats`) and `spinlock_stats` (`sys.dm_os_spinlock_stats`) but had **no viewer surface** (the coverage pin from #1499 allow-listed them as unbuilt UI); this adds one, closing a parity-board Tier-1 gap. New top-level tab (index 13, between Daily Summary and Collection Health, mirroring Darling's diagnostics-tail placement): the latch **wait-time trend** (ms/sec) and spinlock **collisions trend** (collisions/sec) for the TOP 5 contenders stack vertically, each above a collapsed Expander holding its per-class latest-snapshot grid. The reader (`LocalDataService.LatchSpinlock.cs`) mirrors the Darling Postgres reads adapted to DuckDB against the `v_latch_stats` / `v_spinlock_stats` archive views â€” per-second rates computed in SQL from the per-contender `LAG` interval, the same `date_trunc`/`extract(epoch)`/`LAG` idiom the Wait Stats trend uses (the delta tables carry no stored `sample_interval_seconds`). The tab (`ServerTab.LatchSpinlock.cs`) mirrors Darling's rendering with Lite's chart idioms (shared `ChartStyle`/`ChartHoverHelper`, cycling `SeriesColors`, Y-floor-at-0, `UtcOffsetMinutes` display conversion, zero-line-when-empty), wired into the TabControl + `RefreshVisibleTabAsync` switch + `DataGridFilterManager`s for both grids. Removes `latch_stats` / `spinlock_stats` from the coverage-pin allow-list (`CollectorViewerCoverageTests.KnownStoreOnlyOrUnbuiltTables`, now 5), so the ratchet REQUIRES the reader.
- **Parity "net" test guards, round 2 (Tier 0): three cross-app PIN tests** ([#1501]) â€” three more pure-test additions (no runtime behavior change) that extend the "make Lite<->Darling drift a red build" net. All live in `Lite.Tests` (a project BOTH CI workflows run) and read the cross-app half from the source tree â€” the walk-up-from-`bin` technique `SharedCollectorDefaultsPinTests` already uses in CI â€” rather than pull a heavy WPF/service assembly across the SKU boundary (which would churn a locked-mode `packages.lock.json`); each source parser is self-validated against the real in-memory object so a parser bug fails loudly instead of green-washing drift. (1) A **cross-app preset-VALUE pin** (`CrossAppPresetValuePinTests`): the two existing per-app preset pins each check only MEMBERSHIP against a local hardcoded 27-collector set, so a frequency edit on one side only (e.g. Darling Aggressive `plan_cache_stats` 2->3) ships green on both â€” this asserts, per preset (Aggressive/Balanced/Low-Impact), that Lite's `ScheduleManager.s_presets` (the real object) and Darling's `CollectorSchedulePresets.Presets` (parsed from source) are IDENTICAL in collector keys AND minute values. Byte-identical today. (2) An **MCP tool-inventory pin** (`CrossAppMcpToolInventoryPinTests`): nothing pinned the two apps' `[McpServerTool]` sets together (`McpSchemaCompatTests` guards the Gemini schema SHAPE, not the tool set), so the 55-vs-73 tool drift was invisible to CI â€” this asserts Darling's tool set is a SUPERSET of Lite's, with a `KnownLiteMissingMcpTools` RATCHET holding the 18 Darling-only tools Lite hasn't ported yet (latch/spinlock, plan-cache-bloat, cpu-scheduler-pressure, resource-semaphore, default-trace, daily-summary, server/database/trace-flag config-change history, and the 8 `system_health` parser tools) plus a `KnownNamingDrift` allow-list for the one same-capability/different-name pair (Lite `get_blocked_process_reports` <-> Darling `get_blocking`, flagged for a later naming-unification pass). A NEW tool on either side with no twin fails; porting a ratchet tool to Lite forces its removal from the list. (3) A **dispatch-name hardening pin** added to `CollectorGateSurfacePinTests` (from the #1500 gate-surface-collapse review): because the pre-dispatch `CollectorCatalog.AppliesTo(name)` returns true-on-miss for an unknown name, a collector string in Lite's `RunCollectorAsync` switch that drifted from its definition's `Name` would make that target gate silently no-op â€” this scans the switch's case labels from `RemoteCollectorService.cs` and asserts every one is a real `CollectorCatalog` name, with a companion non-vacuous test proving a fake name IS flagged. All three green today, and verified adversarially: injecting the exact `plan_cache_stats` 2->3 drift and a fake Darling-only MCP tool each turn the build red.
- **Parity "net" test guards (Tier 0): collector-coverage pin + Lite<->Darling theme pin** ([#1499]) â€” two pure-test additions (no runtime behavior change) that turn silent Lite<->Darling drift into a red build. (1) A **collector-coverage pin** in both `Lite.Tests` (`CollectorViewerCoverageTests`) and `Darling.Tests` (`ViewerCollectorCoverageTests`) enumerates every `CollectorCatalog` table and asserts each is read by that app's data-service layer (`LocalDataService*.cs` / `ViewerDataService*.cs`) â€” directly or via its `v_` view â€” or is on an explicit `KnownStoreOnlyOrUnbuiltTables` allow-list; a NEW collector table with no viewer reader now fails CI instead of silently collecting-but-never-showing. Darling reads all 35 catalog tables (empty allow-list); Lite's allow-list grandfathers the 7 currently-unbuilt tabs as tracked, shrinking debt (`latch_stats`, `spinlock_stats`, `cpu_scheduler_stats`, `plan_cache_stats`, `session_summary_stats`, `system_health_events`, `default_trace_events`) â€” a companion test fails the moment any of them gains a reader, forcing the entry's removal. (2) A **Lite<->Darling theme-parity pin** in `Lite.Tests` (`ThemeParityLiteDarlingTests`) â€” a project BOTH CI workflows run, unlike the pre-existing `Dashboard.Tests/ThemeParityTests`, which no workflow builds â€” compares the shared `Themes/{Dark,Light,CoolBreeze}Theme.xaml` color keys between the two active apps and fails on any color drift. The palettes are identical today (Lite's 64 color keys are a subset of Darling's, which adds only the 8 viewer-only `Viewer*`/`CardBorder` brushes), so the pin is green and guards forward. Also hardens the existing `ThemeCompletenessTests` (both apps) to exclude `obj`/`bin` by whole path segment rather than substring, so a XAML file whose name embeds `obj`/`bin` can no longer be silently skipped from token-resolution checks.
- **Retained fleet-wide "Job History" tab (Lite + Darling)** â€” a new landing-page tab, right after Alert History, showing retained SQL Agent job-run history across ALL monitored instances ([#1489]). A new shared `JobHistoryCollector` snapshots `msdb.dbo.sysjobhistory` incrementally â€” every step row AND the job-outcome row â€” into a retained `job_history` store, deduped on the `instance_id` high-water mark (a monotonic identity that survives server-side `sp_purge_jobhistory`, so collection is exact-and-complete), with 365-day retention at a 5-minute cadence. The tab offers Server / Job / Status / Category / date filters, per-step rows with retries and durations, per-column filter popups, CSV export, and color-coding for failures (red), retries (yellow), and long runtimes (amber, computed reader-side per job from its own successful-run baseline), plus each job's last successful run. Not collected on Azure SQL DB (no SQL Agent); collects on Managed Instance and AWS RDS. **Phase 2 â€” Agent status + alert:** a companion `agent_status` collector surfaces the SQL Agent service state (Running/Stopped, from `sys.dm_server_services`) and the next scheduled run (from `msdb.dbo.sysjobschedules`) in the tab header, and Darling's headless service raises a new edge-triggered **"Agent Not Running"** self-alert (with an "Agent Restarted" recovery) through the existing self-alert delivery/cooldown path â€” the silent failure nobody notices on an unwatched 24/7 collector. Additive numeric-watermark support was added to the shared collector framework (`NumericWatermark` / `NumericWatermarkColumn`, a `BigInt` parameter type, and a `GetLastCollectedInstanceIdAsync` in both runners) without changing any existing collector's behavior.
- **Global per-server database filter** ([#1490]) -- a viewer-wide, per-server `Databases` toolbar filter (search + Select/Clear All popup, mirroring the wait-type picker) scopes every database-scoped surface -- query grids, blocking, per-database config, trends, slicers, heatmap -- to a chosen subset; server-global views (CPU/memory/waits/jobs/File I/O) and FinOps are unaffected. Applied as SQL push-down below any `LIMIT` (Lite: dynamic positional `IN`; Darling: guarded `AND ($n::text[] IS NULL OR database_name = ANY($n))`), deadlocks + severe-errors filtered client-side, empty selection = All. Persisted per-server. Lite + Darling

- **Darling: "Get Actual Plan" (service-mediated), matching the Dashboard's surfaces** â€” the viewer can now capture an ACTUAL execution plan (with runtime statistics) by re-executing the stored query on the target through the service's new `execute_actual_plan` command. It is available everywhere the Dashboard offers it: Top Queries, the Query-Stats and Query-Store history windows, the Wait drill-down, and the FinOps High Impact grid (procedures stay View-Plan-only â€” re-executing a proc without parameter values is unsafe). The command payload carries an identifier ONLY (per surface: query_hash, query_id, or a snapshot's collection_time + session_id) and NEVER SQL text â€” the service resolves the query text from its own store by that key â€” so the command plane cannot be used to run arbitrary SQL against a monitored server. An informed-consent dialog runs first and PROMINENTLY flags data-modifying queries (INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/MERGE, detected from the plan XML, fail-safe to "modifying" when the plan can't be analyzed) before re-executing. Permission-denied and timeout failures surface a clear message; read-only viewer seats cannot trigger it. (The FinOps Expensive Queries grid is excluded: its rows are grouped by text and carry no store key to resolve re-executable text by.)
- **Shared `ActualPlanExecutor` and `QueryModificationDetector`** â€” the actual-plan executor that was duplicated in Lite and the Dashboard is now a single shared implementation in `PerformanceMonitor.PlanAnalysis` (parameterized by product name), and Lite's existing "Get Actual Plan" consent dialog now flags data-modifying queries too using the shared detector.
- **Darling: managed Postgres derives a memory-tuning `postgresql.conf` block from host RAM â€” scale-readiness for the up-to-500-servers store** ([#1478]) â€” the bundled managed-Postgres bootstrap generated only the TimescaleDB preload/port block and the [#1463] worker-sizing block, leaving the store on PostgreSQL's stock `shared_buffers=128MB` / `work_mem=4MB` / `maintenance_work_mem=64MB` / `effective_cache_size=4GB`. That is genuinely fine at current scale â€” a HammerDB volume pass on an 8 GB VM with 5 monitored servers showed 0 temp-file spills and a 99.3% cache hit, so this is **not** a fix for observed pressure â€” but the stock defaults would bottleneck the "up to 500 servers" target. A new independently-marked `v3` block, appended by `EnsureConfAppended` the SAME marker-guarded way as the worker sizing, now derives from total physical RAM (read at runtime via the Win32 `GlobalMemoryStatusEx`, injectable as a `long` for tests exactly as the worker count derives from the hypertable count): `shared_buffers = min(25% RAM, 8GB)` (restart-only), `effective_cache_size = 75% RAM` (a planner hint, not an allocation), `maintenance_work_mem = min(5% RAM, 1GB)`, and a deliberately-modest per-connection `work_mem = clamp(RAM/512, 16MB, 64MB)` â€” on the 8 GB box that is `shared_buffers 2048MB` / `effective_cache_size 6144MB` / `maintenance_work_mem 409MB` / `work_mem 16MB`, with the caps engaging at 32â€“64 GB. `work_mem` is the conservative one by design (per-sort-per-connection, so worst-case â‰ˆ `max_connections Ã— sorts Ã— work_mem`): the value spills to temp files rather than OOMing, and the store runs a handful of pooled connections rather than 100 concurrent analytical queries. Because `EnsureConfAppended` runs on every start and each marker is checked independently, an **already-provisioned cluster gains the block on its next service-owned restart** (not just a fresh initdb) â€” `shared_buffers` is restart-only but the service restart is a Postgres restart, byte-identical to the worker-sizing block's application path. `StorageVersion` is unchanged (a conf change, not a schema migration) and no live store is touched. Covered by tests mirroring the worker-sizing test: derived values pinned for 8/16/32/64 GB with the caps/clamps engaging, the conf-block content, a non-positive-RAM fallback to a conservative 4 GB, and the gated bootstrap E2E extended to assert the `v3` block is present, live (`work_mem`/`shared_buffers` off their stock defaults), and idempotent.
- **MCP: `get_analysis_findings` and `analyze_server` now return the full copy-paste remediation command (Lite + Darling)** ([#1475]) â€” an AI agent triaging over MCP now gets the SAME runnable T-SQL a human sees on the viewer card. Both read-only analysis tools gained an additive `remediation_command` field, rendered from the finding's PERSISTED `RemediationAction` via the shared `FactRemediation.RenderCopyPasteCommand` â€” all seven remediation shapes (force-plan, clear-plan, server-config, RCSI, safe DB-config, file-growth, missing-index), with the two-sided risk-disclosure comment header riding along on the two destructive shapes (RCSI, clear-plan). It is byte-identical across editions (one shared renderer) and, crucially, renders on `get_analysis_findings` where the older drill-down-sourced `suggested_remediation_sql` was always null: the drill-down is ephemeral and gone on read-back, but the persisted action survives (the [#1473] / [#1474] round-trip). `suggested_remediation_sql` stays unchanged on `analyze_server` (ADDITIVE, non-breaking â€” the two render different text: the older field is drill-down-annotated 3-shape SQL, the new one is bare-runnable 7-shape). `get_analysis_facts` intentionally does NOT carry it â€” it returns raw scored facts, which have no `RemediationAction`. STRICTLY read-only / PRODUCE-ONLY: the MCP renders advisory text and NEVER executes anything, adds no execution/apply/kill path. Both tool descriptions and both editions' MCP instructions document the new field; no tool count changes (a field addition, not a new tool). Covered by a new ungated Lite envelope test (a persisted destructive RCSI action renders the command WITH the two-sided risk header through the tool's JSON; a non-remediable finding renders null) and the Darling gated e2e extended with the same assertions.
- **Lite: recommendation cards now produce the SAME runnable copy-paste T-SQL command the Darling viewer does â€” killing an advise-only-vs-command drift** ([#1474]) â€” Lite has a direct connection to SQL Server, so it is arguably the edition best suited to hand the operator a command they can run; but its Recommendations cards were advise-only, showing prose with no runnable statement, because the copy-paste came from `FactRemediation.GenerateForFinding` which reads the finding's ephemeral drill-down â€” and Lite persisted findings BEFORE enriching them, so on the read-back path the drill-down was gone and the command was always null. This ports [#1473]'s exact pattern: Lite's `AnalysisService` now mirrors Darling's `DarlingAnalysisService` â€” mute-filter into surviving findings, enrich them with drill-down, **build** each finding's `RemediationAction` from the still-present drill-down via the SAME shared `FactRemediation.BuildAction`/`BuildRcsiAction`/`BuildClearPlanAction`/`BuildFileAutogrowthAction`/`BuildServerConfigAction`/`BuildMissingIndexAction` chain, THEN insert â€” persisting the typed action as a new nullable `remediation_action_json` column on DuckDB `analysis_findings` (analysis-schema v4, added idempotently to existing databases by the `ALTER TABLE â€¦ ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` upgrade path, no data loss) via the shared `AlertContextSerializer.SerializeAction`. On read-back the store deserializes it and the card sources its command from the shared `FactRemediation.RenderCopyPasteCommand` â€” the SAME renderer the Darling viewer's Copy-fix delegates to â€” so Lite and Darling produce **byte-identical** commands for all seven remediation shapes (force-plan, clear-plan, server-config, RCSI, safe DB-config, file-growth, missing-index; the two destructive shapes carry the two-sided risk-disclosure comment header automatically). The live `GenerateForFinding` remains only as a fallback for a finding still carrying its drill-down. **No in-app Apply/execute path** â€” Lite produces a COPYABLE command only (the existing Copy button is null-gated on the command, so it appears automatically when present); SQL-side remediation execution stays Dashboard-only per project scope. Covered by real-DuckDB tests (the typed action round-trips through persistence and renders the command on read-back; the v3â†’v4 upgrade adds the column while preserving existing rows and is idempotent) plus per-shape renderer pins mirroring Darling's `ViewerRecommendationsTests`
- **Darling Viewer: a runnable copy-paste T-SQL command for EVERY remediable recommendation, since the headless viewer has no in-app "Apply Fix"** ([#1473]) â€” the viewer reads through the Postgres store and deliberately ships no remediation executor, so a copy-paste command IS its remediation surface. Previously only three of the seven remediation shapes rendered a "Copy fix" command (percent-autogrowth `MODIFY FILE`, missing-index `CREATE`, safe DB-config `ALTER DATABASE â€¦ SET`); findings whose persisted action was **force-plan**, **server-config** (MAXDOP/CTFP/max-min memory), **RCSI**, or **clear-plan** offered neither an Apply button (there is no executor) nor a command â€” a dead end. All seven now render. The renderers live in a single shared entry point, `FactRemediation.RenderCopyPasteCommand(RemediationAction)` in the shared Analysis lib, so the viewer, the Dashboard reader, and the email/MCP surfaces cannot drift and every statement is built by the same `BuildModifyFileStatement` / `BuildSpConfigureStatement` / `StatementFor` shared builders. **force-plan** emits a standalone `USE [db];` + `EXEC sys.sp_query_store_force_plan` block per target (the proc is database-scoped, so each block carries its own `USE` and the copy-paste runs without an ambient database); **server-config** emits `EXEC sys.sp_configure N'â€¦', N; RECONFIGURE;` per setting via the shared builder (including the advise-only memory settings â€” the operator gets the scaffold regardless); **RCSI** emits `ALTER DATABASE [db] SET READ_COMMITTED_SNAPSHOT ON;` per database; **clear-plan** emits a self-contained resolve-and-free script that live-resolves the currently-cached `plan_handle`(s) for the abnormal `query_hash` from `sys.dm_exec_query_stats` (with a review `SELECT` of the affected plans first, since a query hash is not unique to one query) and `DBCC FREEPROCCACHE`s each. The two **destructive** shapes (RCSI, clear-plan) prepend their two-sided `FactRiskDisclosure` text â€” the risk of changing AND of not changing, filled with the finding's real blocking/deadlock/reader-writer or per-exec-CPU figures â€” as a `/* â€¦ */` comment header, so the copy-paste itself discloses the trade-off both ways. The card's existing "Copy fix" button (already gated on a non-null command) now appears for the four newly-rendering shapes with no XAML change; no Apply button or executor is added to the viewer. Validation: full `Darling.Tests` suite green (1943 passed) including per-shape rendering pins (the runnable statement shape + the disclosure comment header on RCSI/clear-plan); the additive shared method compiles and its consumers pass across Lite (41) and Dashboard (346) with no signature change.
- **Darling Viewer: per-server alert badge with Acknowledge (ack-until-worse), so you can see at a glance which servers need attention** ([#1470]) â€” ports Lite's per-tab alert badge + `AlertStateService` into the headless viewer, adapted DuckDBâ†’Postgres and reusing the existing `config_alert_log` poll that already feeds the tray toasts (one read drives both the badges and the toasts â€” no second query, no parallel alert-state store). A count badge appears on each **per-server tab header** (left-click or right-click to acknowledge) and on the **sidebar server row** (count + an "Acknowledge Alerts" context-menu item), colored via the shared `AlertMetricClassifier` (criticalâ†’`ErrorBrush`, else `WarningBrush`) to mirror Lite's deadlockâ†’red / elseâ†’orange-red, capped at "99+", with a metric-breakdown tooltip. `ServerAttentionDeriver` (pure) groups the polled rows per server and **excludes muted rows** (a silenced server shows no badge) and resolution notices; `ViewerAlertStateService` (viewer-local, keyed on the Postgres `server_id`, persisted to `%APPDATA%\PerformanceMonitorDarling\alert-ack-state.json`) holds the **ack-until-worse** state â€” an acknowledgement quiets the badge until a genuinely newer alert (`alert_time` past the ack) arrives, surviving a restart. **Silence stays the store-side whole-server mute rule** (not duplicated â€” the badge respects it by dropping muted rows); **Acknowledge is the new viewer-local gesture** that quiets the badge *without* stopping the service's email/Teams/Slack delivery, kept separate from `AlertToastCoordinator` but consistent with its "newer `alert_time` clears the watermark" semantics â€” exactly as Lite keeps `AlertStateService` separate from its toast/deliverer path. Ack state persists across a tab close (the sidebar badge is always present) and is cleared only when the server is removed; badges paint at startup from the same rows read for toast-priming. Validation: the viewer builds clean and the full `Darling.Tests` suite is green (1935 passed), including `ViewerAlertStateServiceTests` (Lite's `AlertBadgeAckTests` ack-until-worse coverage + a persistence round-trip) and `ServerAttentionDeriverTests` (grouping, muted/resolution exclusion, critical flag, newest-event, tooltip).
- **Shared `system_health_events` collector (Stage 1: raw `system_health` Extended Events capture), so both Lite and Darling collect them** ([#1262]) â€” the first half of the `system_health` parity port: a net-new shared collector that reads the built-in always-on `system_health` XE session's ring buffer and lands each event as **raw XML, one row per event, with no shredding** (`event_time`, `event_type`, `event_xml`). The monitor-side parse into the health-parser warning categories is **Stage 2** (a separate later change) â€” this stage only captures and stores. `SystemHealthEventsCollector` mirrors `DeadlocksCollector`'s capture pattern (`TRY_CAST(target_data AS xml)` from `sys.dm_xe_session_targets` + `sys.dm_xe_sessions`, `.nodes()` split, an `event_time` watermark with a 10-minute first-run fallback so the rolling ring buffer is never re-collected) but with **no session lifecycle** â€” `system_health` is created and started by the Database Engine and must not be altered, so it is only read. **Events captured** (verified live on SQL 2016â€“2025 and against Erik's `sp_HealthParser` event map â€” the clear feeders for the unique warning categories): `sp_server_diagnostics_component_result` (memory conditions / IO / system health), `error_reported` (severe errors), `memory_broker_ring_buffer_recorded` (memory broker), `memory_node_oom_ring_buffer_recorded` (memory node OOM), `scheduler_monitor_system_health_ring_buffer_recorded` (scheduler / non-yielding issues), and `wait_info` (significant individual long waits â€” discrete threshold-crossing events, **not** the cumulative `sys.dm_os_wait_stats` the wait_stats collector aggregates). `xml_deadlock_report` is deliberately **not** captured here (`DeadlocksCollector` already owns deadlocks); `security_error_ring_buffer_recorded` / `connectivity_ring_buffer_recorded` are skipped (outside these categories, and excluded by `sp_HealthParser` too). **Azure verified against MS Learn (not assumed):** the collector **does not apply to Azure SQL Database** â€” MS Learn is explicit that "There's no built-in `system_health` Extended Event session in Azure SQL Database" (the session's *Applies to* lists only SQL Server + Azure SQL Managed Instance), and the captured events are server-level with no database-scoped equivalent to recreate, so unlike the deadlock collector there is nothing to read or stand up there; Erik's own `sp_HealthParser` hard-refuses on Azure SQL DB for the same reason. It is gated `AppliesTo = !IsAzureSqlDb`, still collecting on SQL Server, Azure SQL Managed Instance, and AWS RDS (all of which have the full server-scoped session). Wired end-to-end via `CollectorCatalog` + `CollectorScheduleDefaults` (5-minute cadence / 30-day retention), from which Darling's dispatch, TimescaleDB hypertable conversion, and retention purge flow automatically. **Lite** gets the DuckDB table (`Schema.cs`), schema migration **v40**, archive-view + parquet-purge registration, the collection dispatch, and `ScheduleManager` defaults + all three presets (2/5/15). **Darling** gets migration **V13** (`StorageVersion` 12â†’13) creating the `collect`-schema table (generated from the definition, so column-identical to a fresh store) plus its `v_system_health_events` passthrough view, and the worker dispatch entry. Validation: full Lite + ungated Darling suites green, including a new collector-definition parity pin (server-scoped ring-buffer read, the exact six-event capture filter and the deadlock/security/connectivity exclusions, the watermark + 10-minute fallback, the Azure-SQL-DB skip, the three-column raw-XML payload, null-tolerant ReadAsync/WritePayload), a DuckDB append round-trip pin, and the generated-DDL pin for the `system_health_event_id` prefix / `text` XML mapping.
- **Darling Viewer: the System Events tab gains the CPU Tasks and I/O Issues sub-tabs (`system_health` parity, Stage 2b)** ([#1262]) â€” the two `sp_server_diagnostics`-component categories the full Dashboard's `sp_HealthParser` surfaces that the Darling viewer still lacked, added the same **parse-on-read** way as the existing six (no collector, migration, or persisted-table change). The shared Common `SystemHealthParser` now also shreds the **QUERY_PROCESSING** component into a `CpuTasksRecord` (`maxWorkers` / `workersCreated` / `workersIdle` / `tasksCompletedWithinInterval` / `pendingTasks` / `oldestPendingTaskWaitingTime` / `hasUnresolvableDeadlockOccurred` / `hasDeadlockedSchedulersOccurred`, plus `didBlockingOccur` from `.exist()` over the event's `blockingTasks/blocked-process-report`) and the **IO_SUBSYSTEM** component into `IoIssuesRecord` rows (`ioLatchTimeouts` / `intervalLongIos` / `totalLongIos`, then one row per pending-request file with the `@duration` values summed â€” a faithful port of `sp_HealthParser`'s `#io â†’ #i` `GROUP BY filePath` shred, dropping events with no pending request exactly as its `WHERE duration IS NOT NULL` does). All three sp_server_diagnostics categories (Memory Conditions, CPU Tasks, I/O Issues) read the single captured `sp_server_diagnostics_component_result` feed and split on the event's component, each gating on its own node so one event yields a record for its component alone. `SystemEventSignificance` re-applies `sp_HealthParser`'s warnings-only predicates at the product's canonical parameters â€” **CPU Tasks** keeps `state = WARNING` **and** `pendingTasks >= 10` (`@pending_task_threshold` default), **I/O Issues** keeps `state = WARNING`. Two grid sub-tabs are appended to the Darling System Events strip (grids in Darling's style, not the Dashboard's counter charts) with the shared column filters + per-sub-tab count/refresh. The remaining two Dashboard categories â€” Corruption Events and Contention Events â€” stay out of scope (they need collection work, not a parser add). Validation: `PerformanceMonitor.Common` + the Darling viewer build clean in Release; full Darling.Tests green, including new `SystemHealthParser` column-shred fixtures (a WARNING `queryProcessing` event with blocking; an `ioSubsystem` event whose same-file pending requests sum and whose two files fan out to two rows), the per-component dispatch/no-op pins, and `SystemEventSignificance` threshold pins (the `pendingTasks >= 10` boundary and the `state = WARNING` gate).
- **Shared `cpu_scheduler_stats` + `plan_cache_stats` collectors, so both Lite and Darling collect them** ([#1262]) â€” the second batch of the Dashboard-only DMV collectors the headless Darling service was missing, ported monitor-side (raw DMV, no dependency on a target stored proc) into the shared `PerformanceMonitor.Collectors` layer so one definition drives both SKUs. Unlike the latch/spinlock batch these are **point-in-time snapshots, not cumulative-counter deltas** â€” no delta groups, values read directly (mirroring `MemoryStatsCollector` / `SessionStatsCollector`). `CpuSchedulerStatsCollector` (the Dashboard-parity port of `install/17_collect_cpu_scheduler_stats.sql`) aggregates `sys.dm_os_schedulers` (VISIBLE ONLINE) with single-row `CROSS JOIN`s to `sys.dm_os_sys_info`, `sys.dm_os_sys_memory`, a `sys.dm_os_nodes` NUMA rollup, and a `sys.dm_resource_governor_workload_groups` rollup plus an `OUTER APPLY` over `sys.dm_exec_requests` â€” one row of worker/runnable/blocked/queued task counts, worker-thread exhaustion, NUMA online/offline, OS-memory pressure, and the six Dashboard pressure-warning flags (kept as the proc's own `CONVERT(bit, CASE â€¦)` so the stored values are identical; the reader gets clean booleans). `PlanCacheStatsCollector` (the port of `install/35_collect_plan_cache_stats.sql`) aggregates `sys.dm_exec_cached_plans` by `cacheobjtype`/`objtype` into single-use vs multi-use plan/size bloat with `oldest_plan_create_time` from `sys.dm_exec_query_stats`. The proc `CASE`/`COUNT_BIG`â†’typed-column conversions the Dashboard did implicitly on `INSERT` are made explicit in the query so the positional readers get the exact storage types; the two averages and `runnable_percent` are `decimal(38,2)` matching the Dashboard columns (DuckDB `DECIMAL(38,2)` / Postgres `numeric(38,2)`). **Azure verified against MS Learn (not assumed):** `plan_cache_stats` collects everywhere (`sys.dm_exec_cached_plans` + `sys.dm_exec_query_stats` are on all platforms; on Azure SQL DB the cache is inherently connected-DB-scoped, a platform property not a cut). `cpu_scheduler_stats` **does not apply to Azure SQL Database** â€” `sys.dm_os_sys_memory` is not exposed there (MS Learn "Applies to" lists only SQL Server + Managed Instance) *and* `sys.dm_exec_requests` is limited to the current connection on Azure SQL DB (`VIEW SERVER STATE` can't be granted), so it is gated `AppliesTo = !IsAzureSqlDb` exactly like `memory_pressure_events`, skipping the cycle on Azure SQL DB while still collecting on SQL Server and Azure SQL Managed Instance (which expose the full DMV set). Wired end-to-end via `CollectorCatalog` + `CollectorScheduleDefaults` (cpu_scheduler 1-minute, plan_cache 5-minute, 30-day retention â€” matching the Dashboard's own schedule), from which Darling's dispatch, TimescaleDB hypertable conversion, and retention purge flow automatically. **Lite** gets the two DuckDB tables (`Schema.cs`), schema migration **v38**, archive-view + parquet-purge registration, the collection dispatch, and `ScheduleManager` defaults + all three presets (cpu_scheduler 1/1/5, plan_cache 2/5/15). **Darling** gets migration **V11** (`StorageVersion` 10â†’11) creating the two `collect`-schema tables (generated from the definitions, so column-identical to a fresh store) plus their `v_cpu_scheduler_stats` / `v_plan_cache_stats` passthrough views, and the worker dispatch entries. Validation: full Lite + ungated Darling suites green, including new collector-definition parity pins for both (verbatim query, payload order, ReadAsync mapping incl. nullable columns, point-in-time no-delta WritePayload, the Azure skip) and generated-DDL pins for the `boolean` warnings / `numeric(38,2)` averages / `timestamp` mappings.
- **Shared `latch_stats` + `spinlock_stats` collectors, so both Lite and Darling collect them** ([#1262]) â€” the first batch of the Dashboard-only DMV collectors the headless Darling service was missing, ported monitor-side (raw DMV, no dependency on a target stored proc) into the shared `PerformanceMonitor.Collectors` layer so one definition drives both SKUs. `LatchStatsCollector` (`sys.dm_os_latch_stats` â€” per-latch-class `waiting_requests_count` / `wait_time_ms` / `max_wait_time_ms`) and `SpinlockStatsCollector` (`sys.dm_os_spinlock_stats` â€” `collisions` / `spins` / `spins_per_collision` / `sleep_time` / `backoffs`) mirror `WaitStatsCollector` exactly: a single cumulative-counter DMV, delta-based between snapshots via the shared `CollectorDeltaCalculator` â€” which detects a counter reset itself, so the Dashboard proc's `server_start_time` reset marker is dropped (not needed, not a scope-down). Both DMVs are available on SQL Server, Azure SQL Database, and Azure SQL Managed Instance (verified against MS Learn), so they collect everywhere â€” no platform skip, matching wait_stats; `spins_per_collision` is the DMV's `real` ratio, read via `GetFloat` and stored as `double precision` with no delta (it's a computed ratio, mirroring the Dashboard table). Wired end-to-end: added to `CollectorCatalog` and `CollectorScheduleDefaults` (1-minute cadence / 30-day retention, matching the Dashboard's own schedule) â€” from which Darling's collection dispatch, TimescaleDB hypertable conversion, and retention purge all flow automatically. **Lite** gets the two DuckDB tables (`Schema.cs`), schema migration **v37**, archive-view + parquet-purge registration, and the collection dispatch + `ScheduleManager` defaults/presets. **Darling** gets migration **V10** (`StorageVersion` 9â†’10) creating the two `collect`-schema tables (generated from the definitions, so column-identical to a fresh store) plus their `v_latch_stats` / `v_spinlock_stats` passthrough views, and the worker dispatch entry. Two more Dashboard-only DMV collectors (cpu_scheduler, plan_cache) follow in a later batch. Validation: 874 Lite tests + 526 ungated Darling tests green, including new collector-definition parity pins for both and the generated-DDL pin for `spinlock_stats`' `double precision` mapping.
- **Darling security hardening Phase 1: least-privilege schema split + roles, so the Viewer stops connecting as the superuser** ([#1262]) â€” the bundled-managed Postgres store used to keep everything in one flat `public` schema with **every** consumer â€” including the interactive Viewer, which only ever needs to read plus write three small config tables â€” connecting as the `darling` cluster superuser, exactly the shape (a loopback socket handing out a superuser credential) an enterprise scan flags and the reason a buggy Viewer could `DROP` the whole history. Phase 1 closes that. A new **V8 migration** (`StorageVersion` 7â†’8) creates two schemas and moves every object out of `public` with `ALTER â€¦ SET SCHEMA`: **`collect`** holds the 26 collector hypertables plus the service-written / user-read metadata (`servers`, `collection_log`, `analysis_findings`, `darling_schema_version`, the 24 `v_*` views) and is read-only to users; **`config`** holds exactly the four tables a human operator mutates through the Viewer/MCP (`config_mute_rules`, `config_alert_log`, `config_edge_trigger_watermarks`, `analysis_muted`). The move is generated from the collector catalog (`PgSchemaGenerator.GenerateV8Move`), so a future collector migrates for free, and it runs identically on a fresh store (V1â€“V7 make plain tables in `public`, V8 moves them, *then* TimescaleDB converts the now-in-`collect` tables) and on an existing hypertabled store (`ALTER TABLE <hypertable> SET SCHEMA` moves the hypertable and propagates to its chunks). **Table names are unchanged and no query is re-qualified**: the shared SQL keeps its bare table references â€” deliberately byte-identical to Lite's DuckDB SQL, a twinning contract pinned by tests â€” and resolves them through **`search_path = collect, config, public`**, set as the database default (best-effort in `MigrateAsync`, so a least-privilege BYO login that can't `ALTER DATABASE` is warned, not failed) and carried on the managed connection strings. The service provisions **two least-privilege login roles** (`DarlingManagedRoles`, idempotent + self-healing on every start, the marker-guarded conf-append discipline applied to roles): **`admin`** (`LOGIN NOSUPERUSER`, SELECT on both schemas + INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on `config` only â€” the Viewer's default identity) and **`viewer`** (SELECT only), with `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE darling` so new collector tables auto-inherit SELECT, and `REVOKE`/`GRANT CONNECT` hardening that closes `public` without locking the two roles out. Because the role names are the bare, un-prefixed `admin`/`viewer`, provisioning stamps each role it creates with a `darling-managed` marker comment and **fails loud** if a same-named role without that marker already exists (never silently repurposing an unrelated login). Each role gets its own generated **DPAPI-LocalMachine credential** (`pg-admin-credential.dpapi` / `pg-viewer-credential.dpapi`) beside the data directory, same posture as the owner's `pg-credential.dpapi`; a deleted file self-heals (the superuser re-asserts the password, a nicer property than the owner's unrecoverable one). Because DPAPI LocalMachine is machine-bound (the service writes, a different interactive user's Viewer reads) and the entropy is in source, the credential files are now protected by a **restrictive NTFS ACL** (`DarlingFileSecurity`): the data-directory parent and all `*.dpapi` files (plus the transient init pwfile) have inherited world-read stripped, leaving SYSTEM + Administrators + the service account â€” the **superuser** credential and pwfile lock out interactive users entirely (post-split the Viewer never needs them), while the **admin/viewer** credentials additionally grant read to `NT AUTHORITY\INTERACTIVE` (the operator's Viewer, excluding the non-interactive local attack primitives â€” services, SSRF/sandboxed socket code â€” the threat model calls out). An existing credential file is trusted only when a trusted principal owns it (SYSTEM/Administrators/the service account), closing the pre-plant vector where an attacker seeds a role password the `ALTER ROLE` re-assert would then hand them; an untrusted role file is discarded and regenerated. Hardening is best-effort with a loud error (never bricks the service) and re-applied every start. The Viewer drops from superuser to **`admin` by default**, selectable via `darling.json` **`postgres.connectAs`** (`"admin"` | `"viewer"`); a `"viewer"`-role deployment degrades gracefully â€” an authoritative `has_table_privilege` capability probe drives `ViewerDataService.IsReadOnly`, which **hides/disables** the mute-rule Add/Edit/Toggle/Delete/Purge buttons and the alert Dismiss/Dismiss All buttons, backed by a **reactive 42501 catch** that turns a live permission-denied into a clear "read-only connection" message rather than a crash. The service still connects as the owner `darling` for migration, provisioning, and collector writes. **Bring-your-own Postgres is a first-class path**: the schema split runs everywhere (it's a migration), and BYO operators provision the same roles out-of-band with a shipped `Darling/tools/provision-roles.sql`. **Lite's DuckDB store and the Dashboard are untouched** â€” schemas are a Postgres concept, and the bare-name/`search_path` approach is precisely what keeps the twinned SQL from forking (the pin tests stay green). Validation: 480 ungated Darling tests green (V8 shape from the catalog, the provisioning DDL shape + alphanumeric-password guard, the `connectAs` derivation for admin/viewer/unknown, the read-only probe + exception, `BuildConnectionString`/`ViewerSettings` carrying the search path), plus gated live round-trips (`DarlingSecuritySplitLiveTests`) proving the tables really move, `admin` writes / `viewer` is denied (42501) / a new `collect` table auto-grants, and an already-compressed hypertable survives the `SET SCHEMA` move and stays readable by the least-privilege role.
- **Execution-plan capture extended to procedure_stats, blocked_process_report, and deadlocks** ([#1262]) â€” the #1349 plan-capture pattern (the shared `CollectorContext.CapturePlanXml` flag: on for Darling, never set by Lite, and byte-identical SQL when off) now reaches three more collectors so the Darling viewer's deferred View-Plan surfaces have plans to show. `procedure_stats` gains a **clean** `query_plan_xml`: because the module-level DMVs (`sys.dm_exec_procedure_stats`/`_trigger_stats`/`_function_stats`) expose no statement offsets, the whole-module plan is read from `sys.dm_exec_text_query_plan(plan_handle, 0, -1)` â€” still the text DMV, so oversized plans return â€” spliced into all three UNION branches and the Azure variant. `blocked_process_report` (`blocked_query_plan_xml` + `blocking_query_plan_xml`) and `deadlocks` (`victim_query_plan_xml`) get **best-effort** capture: at collection time the collector resolves each contending statement's plan from the plan cache by the executionStack frame's `sql_handle` + statement offsets via `sys.dm_exec_query_stats` â†’ `sys.dm_exec_text_query_plan` â€” the exact lookup the apps' live #880 right-click View Plan already uses â€” walking frames top-of-stack first. Honestly best-effort: the XE events fire asynchronously, so a plan may have aged out of cache by collection, and dynamic-SQL/adhoc frames carry a zero `sql_handle` that resolves to nothing â€” either way the column stores NULL rather than a fabricated plan. Lite stays byte-identical with the flag off (proven by the collector-definition pins and a real DuckDB append round-trip); the new nullable columns ripple to both stores â€” Lite DuckDB migration v35 and Darling storage V7 (`ALTER TABLE â€¦ ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`), fresh installs get them from the generated schema. The deprecated Dashboard's hand-maintained install SQL is intentionally untouched.
- **Darling Viewer W1j: the Memory inner tab â€” Lite's four Memory sub-tabs, copied** ([#1262]) â€” the Memory tab Lite carries between CPU and File I/O, reproduced on the W0 shell in that exact position: an inner sub-TabControl with **Overview** (a ten-tile summary strip â€” physical/target/buffer-pool/plan-cache/page-file/state/model â€” over the Total-vs-Target-vs-Buffer-Pool memory trend with a memory-grants overlay), **Memory Clerks** (a checkbox picker mirroring the wait/perfmon pickers â€” Top-5 default, search filter, clear-only-the-filtered-visible-set, the reentrancy + generation guards â€” driving a batched per-clerk trend plus a non-buffer-pool total/top-clerk summary), **Memory Grants** (per-resource-pool sizing and activity charts), and **Memory Pressure Events** (hour-bucketed stacked SQL-vs-OS medium/severe pressure bars, geometry copied verbatim from Lite on the shared `ChartPalette` pressure keys). Reads rewired to Postgres in `ViewerDataService.Memory.cs` against the `v_memory_stats` / `v_memory_grant_stats` / `v_memory_clerks` / `v_memory_pressure_events` passthrough views â€” the `numeric(18,2)` MB columns CAST to `double precision` and the grant count SUMs to `bigint` for the typed readers â€” reusing W1d's `GetMemoryTrendAsync` for the Overview trend rather than duplicating it; the two clerk/pressure views V4/V5 had skipped are created by a new migration (V6 `memory-tab-passthrough-views`, `StorageVersion` bumped to 6). Deferred: the sub-tab-only timer refresh (the tab loads all four sub-tabs on activation, Lite's full-refresh branch), and a de-skew for the server-local pressure `sample_time` (like CPU's #1262 skew, but the CPU per-batch de-skew assumes a dense ring buffer and does not transfer to the sparse resource monitor â€” the standalone chart stays internally consistent and shifts only for non-UTC servers)
- **PerformanceMonitor.Collectors: the shared collector-definition library â€” ALL 26 of Lite's collectors extracted** (wait_stats, tempdb_stats, memory_grant_stats, cpu_utilization, memory_stats, memory_clerks, memory_pressure_events, file_io_stats, server_properties, server_config, database_config, trace_flags, database_scoped_config, session_stats, waiting_tasks, procedure_stats, running_jobs, perfmon_stats, dmv_blocking_snapshot, database_size_stats, index_object_stats, query_stats, query_snapshots, query_store, deadlocks, blocked_process_report) ([#1262]) â€” the parity keystone of the headless plan: a collector definition owns the T-SQL sent to monitored servers, the result-row mapping, the delta rules (groups, keys, gap policy), and the payload column order, so portable Lite (DuckDB appender) and the future Darling service (Postgres COPY) run the SAME monitoring brain and a collector change lands exactly once. Lite's wait_stats collector now runs through the shared `WaitStatsCollector` definition via a generic runner that reproduces the hand-rolled loop byte-for-byte at the storage layer (standard prefix columns, separated SQL/storage phase timing from [#1180], ignored-wait filtering from [#1240]). The contract is now unit-testable without a live SQL Server: `WaitStatsCollectorDefinitionTests` pins the verbatim query text, the filter, the delta groups/keys/300-second gap policy, and the payload order â€” an intentional change must consciously update the pin. Batch 2 (tempdb_stats, memory_grant_stats) proved two more collector shapes on the same contract â€” multi-result-set-to-one-row (tempdb's two SELECTs, still emitting a zero row when empty) and composite delta keys (`{pool}_{semaphore}`) â€” and added `short` to the writer surface. Batch 3 (cpu_utilization) grew the contract to target-aware queries: definitions now build their query per cycle (`BuildQuery(context)`) with bound parameters, can declare a **watermark column** the host reads from its own store before the cycle (Azure filters `end_time` server-side; the ring buffer dedups client-side because its `sample_time` is computed), and the target info carries the Azure-SQL-DB flag â€” preserving the Linux NULL-other-CPU rule ([#1048]) and the incomplete-SystemHealth skip ([#989]) verbatim. One deliberate metrics nuance: the fetch/store timing split ([#1180]) now uniformly attributes result-row reading to the SQL (fetch) phase for every migrated collector â€” cpu_utilization, memory_clerks, memory_pressure_events, session_stats, waiting_tasks, and procedure_stats previously counted row reads in the storage phase as a streaming artifact, so their reported sql_ms rises and duck_ms falls correspondingly; collected monitoring data is unaffected. Batch 4 (the three memory collectors) added the last general contract piece: definitions declare `AppliesTo(target)` so collectors that don't exist on a platform skip the whole cycle (memory_pressure_events on Azure SQL DB, where ring buffers aren't exposed), and memory_stats carries the [#857] elastic-pool variant (committed-target approximation, NULL worker count) verbatim. Batch 5 (file_io_stats) added the per-database execution mode â€” Azure SQL DB scopes `dm_io_virtual_file_stats` to the connected database, so definitions can declare `RunsPerDatabase` and the host enumerates databases, opens per-database connections, and aggregates rows (erroring databases skipped and logged, as before) â€” plus the shared `DatabaseExclusionFilter` (the parameterized `NOT IN` splice), pinned by a test asserting it produces byte-identical SQL to Lite's original builder so the temporary duplication cannot drift. Batch 6 (server_properties) added best-effort **supplemental queries** â€” a definition can declare a second query on the same connection whose failure can never fail the primary rows (the WS5 LPIM/IFI/memory-dump health probe, skipped on Azure), moved the Azure vCore parser into the shared definition, and introduced `CollectorDefinitionBase` so new definitions override only what differs from the common case. Batch 7 (server_config, database_config, trace_flags) added **version-gated column selection** â€” `CollectorTargetInfo` now carries the SQL major version and the Managed-Instance flag, and database_config reproduces the original's gates exactly (2019 columns on major â‰¥ 15 / version-unknown / Azure; the 2025 optimized-locking column on major â‰¥ 17; ungated columns write NULL) â€” while trace_flags keeps its permission-tolerant DBCC catch host-side. Batch 8 (database_scoped_config) added the **enumeration shape** â€” the proven `[db].sys.sp_executesql` per-database idiom: a definition lists items first (the on-prem list keeps its AG filter, collecting only from non-AG databases or primary replicas), then runs one query per item on the same connection with bracket-escaped database names, and a failing database is skipped with a warning exactly as before. This is the execution shape the Query Store collector uses, so its extraction is now unblocked. Batch 9 (session_stats, waiting_tasks) needed no new contract shapes â€” the library's shapes now cover the majority of Lite's collection surface. Batch 10 (procedure_stats) carried the dynamic-SQL shape over intact â€” the proc/trigger/function UNION built into an `@sql` variable (spills columns spliced per branch because `dm_exec_function_stats` has none), the exclusion filter interpolated as **double-escaped literals** via the shared `DatabaseExclusionFilter.BuildLiteralClause` (identity-pinned against Lite's original in both escape modes), the Azure single-database variant, and the plan_handle-keyed delta groups with their db.schema.object fallback. Batch 11 (running_jobs) carried the msdb p95-duration comparison over verbatim and pinned the one prefix exception (`running_jobs` has no `collection_id`; definitions now declare `IncludesCollectionId`); the failed-jobs alert query deliberately stays host-side until the Phase-5 alert-engine extraction. A mid-sweep retrospective at 16/25 (mechanical SQL-fidelity audit of every extracted query against the pre-extraction originals + a fresh-context review across runner semantics, payload order, deltas, types, and exception scoping) found **zero blockers or majors**; its three small findings are folded in here: the fetch/store timing note above widened beyond cpu, the supplemental query now skips on an empty primary result exactly like the originals, and `resource_semaphore_id`'s column metadata corrected to a new `SmallInt` type (matters only for future Darling DDL generation). Batch 12 (perfmon_stats, dmv_blocking_snapshot) moved two more pieces of monitoring brain into the shared library: the **curated 59-counter perfmon list** now lives in the definition (the `perfmon_counters.json` override still wins, flowing in via context) with its escaped-literal interpolation and `{object}|{counter}|{instance}` delta contract pinned, and the DMV blocking snapshot carries its layered minimum-wait floors and the **synthetic negative monitor_loop** (seconds since 2020-01-01, negated so DMV episodes can never collide with real BPR loops) verbatim. Batch 13 (database_size_stats) needed nothing new: the server-side cursor + `FILEPROPERTY(SpaceUsed)` staging batch, the two-site exclusion splice (cursor and outer SELECT sharing one parameter set), volume stats, and the Azure per-database variant all rode existing shapes. Batch 14 (index_object_stats â€” the heavy per-index sweep) added the last small contract knob, a **per-collector command timeout** (this collector keeps its dedicated 300-second per-database budget from the [#1135] fix while enumeration stays on the default), preserved the `#temp`-staged sp_IndexCleanup technique and dual execution (Azure per-database connections; on-prem enumeration with the quote-doubled `[db].sys.sp_executesql` body), and unified the per-database loops' error handling to be cancellation-aware: a cancel now always propagates instead of being swallowed as a skipped database, and a non-SQL per-database failure now skips that database with a warning instead of failing the whole cycle (previously only SqlExceptions were tolerated on the enumeration path). Batch 15 (query_stats) preserved the full row-identity delta key (`sql_handle:start:end:plan_handle` â€” the fix for multi-statement plans cross-contaminating each other's deltas), the interval-captured worker delta feeding `sample_interval_seconds`, and both query variants; its previously-overlapping fetch/store stopwatches join the uniform timing attribution documented above. Batch 16 (query_snapshots) moved the running-queries snapshot â€” the CDC-capture detection (Agent job-id parse with the text-match fallback when msdb's cdc_jobs is unreadable), the live-query-plan version gate (2016 SP1+/MI, never Azure SQL DB â€” [#857]), the Azure `#req`-staging variant that keeps the sql_text/plan DMFs away from master-scoped rows ([#857]), and the [#1286] memory-grant/tempdb/transaction triage columns â€” into the shared definition; its query templates now also serve Lite's **live snapshot button** through a one-line shim, so the scheduled collector and the interactive view can never build different SQL. Batch 17 (query_store) rode the batch-8 enumeration shape (the actual_state cursor probe that lists only databases with Query Store genuinely enabled â€” AG-aware on-prem, plain on Azure) plus the last small contract knob, an optional **enumeration probe**: a quick best-effort scalar the host runs between listing items and the per-item loop, because query_store decides its 2017+/2022+ version-gated columns from a **live PRODUCTVERSION check each cycle** (10-second budget, default 13 on failure) rather than trusting cached connection status, which can be version-unknown. The incremental `last_execution_time` watermark (60-minute fallback), the per-database `sp_executesql` query, and the float-to-bigint stat coercion moved verbatim; the collector is the second Nameâ‰ TargetTable case (`query_store` â†’ `query_store_stats`). One deliberate deviation: the per-database DuckDB write-spike breakdown log (reader/append/flush stopwatches behind a 2-second threshold) was diagnostic scaffolding from the Query Store performance investigations and does not carry over â€” the runner's uniform fetch/store phase timing ([#1180]) remains.
